CVE-2025-68670: discovering an RCE vulnerability in xrdp

Securelist (Kaspersky)
2026-05-08 4 use cases 2 techniques 3 kill-chain phases detected

During a security assessment of Kaspersky USB Redirector, we discovered CVE-2025-68670: a pre-auth RCE in the xrdp server component. Project maintainers promptly patched the vulnerability.

[LLM] Vulnerable xrdp version (CVE-2025-68670 pre-auth RCE) on Linux endpoints Exploitation High
Surfaces Linux hosts running xrdp versions affected by CVE-2025-68670, the pre-auth stack overflow in xrdp_wm_parse_domain_information. Patched in xrdp 0.10.5 (and back-ports 0.9.27, 0.10.4.1) — anything below those is exploitable without credentials by any client able to reach TCP/3389. Use this hunt to drive patching priority before alerting controls catch the active exploitation. Rationale: Driven entirely by the article-confirmed CVE ID and the patched-version triplet (0.10.5 / 0.9.27 / 0.10.4.1) named explicitly by the Kaspersky researchers. Defender TVM and Sentinel TVM both natively populate CveId, so no fragile string parsing is needed. Pivot on IsInternetFacing because the bug is pre-auth — internet-exposed xrdp is critical, internal is high. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/neutrinolabs/xrdp/security/advisories
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities.Vulnerabilities
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where CveId =~ "CVE-2025-68670"
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceInfo
    | summarize arg_max(Timestamp, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP, MachineGroup) by DeviceId
  ) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, OSVersion, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP, MachineGroup
| order by IsInternetFacing desc, DeviceName asc
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2d)
| where CveId =~ "CVE-2025-68670"
| summarize arg_max(TimeGenerated, *) by DeviceId, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceInfo
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(2d)
    | summarize arg_max(TimeGenerated, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP, MachineGroup) by DeviceId
  ) on DeviceId
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceName, OSPlatform, OSVersion, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP, MachineGroup
| order by IsInternetFacing desc, DeviceName asc

[LLM] xrdp daemon segfault / overflow log lines — CVE-2025-68670 exploitation indicator Exploitation Medium
Catches the host-side artefact of a CVE-2025-68670 exploitation attempt: xrdp_wm_parse_domain_information overruns a 256-byte stack buffer with up to 512 bytes from a crafted domain name and crashes the xrdp worker (or, on the boundary, leaves the source-file error strings 'ts_info_utf16_in: output buffer overflow' / 'ts_info_utf16_in: bad terminator' in the log). xrdp segfaults are rare in steady state — pairing the daemon name with a crash/overflow string is high-fidelity. Rationale: The two literal log strings 'ts_info_utf16_in: output buffer overflow' and 'ts_info_utf16_in: bad terminator' are taken directly from the xrdp source code shown in the article — they only emit at the boundary the attacker has to cross to deliver the overflow into xrdp_wm_parse_domain_information. Pairing them with kernel segfault messages naming the xrdp binary covers both the 'failed exploit' (overflow logged, no RCE) and 'successful exploit / crash' (segfault) cases. Defender query falls back to xrdp respawn-loop detection because MDE Linux does not emit kernel segfault events. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/neutrinolabs/xrdp/security/advisories
ATT&CKT1190 T1499.004
Data sources
// Linux endpoints: detect xrdp worker respawn loop (proxy for crash-on-exploit)
let WindowMin = 5m;
let RespawnThreshold = 3;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("systemd","init","xrdp","xrdp-sesman")
| where FileName in~ ("xrdp","xrdp-sesman","xrdp-chansrv")
| summarize Respawns = count(),
            FirstSeen = min(Timestamp),
            LastSeen = max(Timestamp),
            Parents = make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName),
            CmdLines = make_set(ProcessCommandLine)
            by DeviceName, DeviceId, bin(Timestamp, WindowMin)
| where Respawns >= RespawnThreshold
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, DeviceId, Respawns, FirstSeen, LastSeen, Parents, CmdLines
| order by Respawns desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where Facility in~ ("kern","daemon","auth","authpriv","user","syslog")
| where SyslogMessage has "xrdp"
| where SyslogMessage has_any (
    "segfault at",
    "general protection",
    "core dumped",
    "ts_info_utf16_in: output buffer overflow",
    "ts_info_utf16_in: bad terminator"
  )
| extend CrashIndicator = extract(@"(segfault at \S+|general protection|core dumped|ts_info_utf16_in:[^""]+)", 1, SyslogMessage)
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostIP, Facility, ProcessName, ProcessID, CrashIndicator, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: xrdp Process Crash or Buffer-Overflow Log Line (CVE-2025-68670)
id: 8e3a1f4a-9b8e-4c1d-89aa-2f9d1e6a4b5c
status: experimental
description: |
  Detects Linux syslog messages that name the xrdp daemon together with a
  segfault, core-dump, or xrdp-internal overflow string. xrdp_wm_parse_domain_information
  in xrdp <0.10.5 / <0.9.27 / <0.10.4.1 contains a pre-auth stack buffer
  overflow (CVE-2025-68670); a remote unauthenticated attacker on TCP/3389
  can crash or take over the worker by sending a Client Info PDU whose
  domain field starts with '_' and exceeds 256 UTF-8 bytes before the
  closing '__'. xrdp does not normally segfault, so this combination is
  high-fidelity.
references:
  - https://securelist.com/cve-2025-68670/119742/
  - https://github.com/neutrinolabs/xrdp/security/advisories
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/08
tags:
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1190
  - attack.impact
  - attack.t1499.004
logsource:
  product: linux
  service: syslog
detection:
  xrdp_named:
    Message|contains: 'xrdp'
  crash_or_overflow:
    Message|contains:
      - 'segfault at'
      - 'general protection'
      - 'core dumped'
      - 'ts_info_utf16_in: output buffer overflow'
      - 'ts_info_utf16_in: bad terminator'
  condition: xrdp_named and crash_or_overflow
falsepositives:
  - xrdp upgrade or graceful restart producing benign daemon-restart messages (rule keys on segfault/overflow strings, so this should be rare)
  - Hardware faults causing genuine kernel segfaults unrelated to RDP
level: high
source:linux.syslog (xrdp OR xrdp-sesman OR xrdp-chansrv) ("segfault at" OR "general protection" OR "core dumped" OR "ts_info_utf16_in: output buffer overflow" OR "ts_info_utf16_in: bad terminator")
APPLICATION
search (index=os OR index=linux OR index=unix) sourcetype IN ("syslog","linux_messages_syslog","linux_secure") "xrdp" ("segfault at" OR "general protection" OR "core dumped" OR "ts_info_utf16_in: output buffer overflow" OR "ts_info_utf16_in: bad terminator")
| rex field=_raw "(?<crash_indicator>segfault at \S+|general protection|core dumped|ts_info_utf16_in:[^\"]+)"
| stats count as crashes, min(_time) as first_seen, max(_time) as last_seen, values(crash_indicator) as crash_indicators, values(_raw) as raw_messages by host
| eval cve="CVE-2025-68670", note="Pre-auth xrdp_wm_parse_domain_information stack overflow — pivot to inbound TCP/3389 src_ip on this host within +/- 5m"
| sort - last_seen

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Registry
// Article-specific bespoke detection — CVE-2025-68670: discovering an RCE vulnerability in xrdp
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("mstsc.exe"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FileName in~ ("mstsc.exe"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-42208 — BerriAI LiteLLM SQL Injection Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-05-08 1 use case 0 techniques 3 kill-chain phases detected

BerriAI LiteLLM contains a SQL injection vulnerability that allows an attacker to read data from the proxy's database and potentially modify it, leading to unauthorized access to the proxy and the credentials it manages. Vendor: BerriAI, Product: LiteLLM. Federal patch due: 2026-05-11.

[LLM] Talos weekly prevalent-malware hash match (Coinminer / Injector / Dropper.Miner) Installation High
Sweep endpoint process, file-write and image-load telemetry for any of the five SHA256s published in the 2026-05-07 Talos Threat Source newsletter as the most prevalent malware files (Win.Worm.Coinminer, W32.Injector, Auto.90B145, MalwareXgenMisc, Win.Dropper.Miner). High-confidence retro-hunt and forward-looking alert because Talos has already attributed each hash to a known family. Rationale: Five SHA256s plus the matching MD5s come straight from the Talos newsletter's 'Most prevalent malware files' block, each with a Talos detection name (Win.Worm.Coinminer::1201, W32.Injector:Gen.21ie.1201, Auto.90B145.282358.in02, W32.Variant:MalwareXgenMisc.29d4.1201, Win.Dropper.Miner::95.sbx.tg). Hashes are non-ambiguous primary IOCs - matching them across process/file/image-load gives an alerting-tier signal with effectively zero false positives. Did not need external corroboration; Talos publishes both the hash and the family name itself.
ATT&CKT1204.002 T1496 T1055

[LLM] Talos dropper filename pattern d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_<N>_Exe.exe Installation Medium
Hunts processes or file writes whose name matches the very distinctive Talos-observed dropper naming scheme '<MD5-of-parent>_<sequential int>_Exe.exe' - specifically the d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274 family that produced both the W32.Injector (_63_Exe.exe) and Win.Dropper.Miner (_62_Exe.exe) variants in the 2026-05-07 prevalence list. Catches as-yet-unseen sequence numbers (e.g. _64_, _65_) of the same campaign even when a fresh sample's hash hasn't propagated to threat intel yet. Rationale: Both Talos sample filenames d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_62_Exe.exe (a31f222f...) and d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_63_Exe.exe (96fa6a77...) share the same 32-hex-prefix + sequential integer + _Exe.exe suffix - this is a unique enough pattern to act as a hunting anchor for sibling samples whose hashes haven't yet been published. The leading hex string is itself the MD5 of an unspecified parent file, and the _Exe.exe (capital E) suffix is highly atypical for benign software. Marked hunting-tier rather than alerting because we have only two confirmed sequence numbers and want analyst review before auto-blocking new ones.
ATT&CKT1036.005 T1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let DropperPattern = @"(?i)^d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_\d+_Exe\.exe$";
union isfuzzy=true
(DeviceProcessEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where FileName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, FileName))
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, Seq, FolderPath, SHA256, MD5, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, Source="ProcessEvents"),
(DeviceFileEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed")
  | where FileName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, FileName))
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, Seq, FolderPath, SHA256, MD5, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="FileEvents")
| order by Timestamp desc
let DropperPattern = @"(?i)d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_\d+_Exe\.exe$";
union isfuzzy=true
(ImProcessCreate
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where TargetProcessName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, TargetProcessName))
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, Seq, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, SHA256, MD5, Source="ImProcessCreate"),
(SecurityEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventID == 4688
  | where NewProcessName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, NewProcessName))
  | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, Seq, CommandLine, ParentProcessName, Source="SecurityEvent_4688"),
(ImFileEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed")
  | where TargetFileName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, TargetFileName))
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFileName, Seq, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256, TargetFileMD5, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, Source="ImFileEvent")
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Talos Dropper Filename Pattern d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_<N>_Exe.exe
id: 4b6e9d12-7c08-4f3a-a4d2-2026050700a2
description: Detects execution of files matching the distinctive Talos-observed dropper naming scheme d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_<seq>_Exe.exe. Two variants (_62_Exe.exe Win.Dropper.Miner, _63_Exe.exe W32.Injector) appeared in the 2026-05-07 Cisco Talos prevalence list; this rule generalises to any sequence number in the same family.
references:
  - https://blog.talosintelligence.com/unplug-your-way-to-better-code/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/07
tags:
  - attack.t1036.005
  - attack.t1204.002

  category: process_creation
  product: windows

    Image|re: '(?i)d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_\d+_Exe\.exe$'
  condition: selection
level: medium

DNS tunneling / TXT-heavy domain queries Command & Control Medium
Long subdomain labels + frequent queries to a single 2LD = DNS C2/exfil.
ATT&CKT1071.004 T1048.003
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemotePort == 53 and isnotempty(RemoteUrl)
| extend qlen = strlen(RemoteUrl)
| where qlen > 50
| extend SecondLevelDomain = extract(@"([\w-]+\.[a-zA-Z]{2,})$", 1, RemoteUrl)
| summarize qcount = count(), uniqueSubs = dcount(RemoteUrl), maxLabel = max(qlen)
    by DeviceName, SecondLevelDomain
| where qcount > 100 and uniqueSubs > 20
| order by qcount desc
// ASIM ImDnsActivity normalises Windows DNS, Sysmon EID 22, Linux dnsmasq, etc.
ImDnsActivity
| where TimeGenerated > ago(24h)
| where DnsQueryTypeName == "TXT"
| summarize Queries = count(), DistinctNames = dcount(DnsQuery)
            by SrcIpAddr, bin(TimeGenerated, 5m)
| where Queries > 200    // tune to TXT-query baseline for environment
| order by Queries desc

    by DNS.src, DNS.query
| `drop_dm_object_name(DNS)`
| eval qlen=len(query)
| where qlen > 50
| rex field=query "(?<second_level_domain>[\w-]+\.[\w-]+)$"
| stats sum(count) AS qcount, dc(query) AS unique_subs, max(qlen) AS max_label
    by src, second_level_domain
| where qcount > 100 AND unique_subs > 20
| sort - qcount
| tstats summariesonly=false count from datamodel=Network_Resolution.DNS
    where DNS.message_type="QUERY"
    by DNS.src, DNS.query
| `drop_dm_object_name(DNS)`
| eval qlen=len(query)
| where qlen > 50
| rex field=query "(?<second_level_domain>[\w-]+\.[\w-]+)$"
| stats sum(count) AS qcount, dc(query) AS unique_subs, max(qlen) AS max_label
    by src, second_level_domain
| where qcount > 100 AND unique_subs > 20
| sort - qcount

// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.

    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("9f1f11a708d393e0a4109ae189bc64f1f3e312653dcf317a2bd406f18ffcc507", "96fa6a7714670823c83099ea01d24d6d3ae8fef027f01a4ddac14f123b1c9974", "90b1456cdbe6bc2779ea0b4736ed9a998a71ae37390331b6ba87e389a49d3d59", "e60ab99da105ee27ee09ea64ed8eb46d8edc92ee37f039dbc3e2bb9f587a33ba", "a31f222fc283227f5e7988d1ad9c0aecd66d58bb7b4d8518ae23e110308dbf91", "2915b3f8b703eb744fc54c81f4a9c67f", "aac3165ece2959f39ff98334618d10d9", "c2efb2dcacba6d3ccc175b6ce1b7ed0a", "dbd8dbecaa80795c135137d69921fdba", "7bdbd180c081fa63ca94f9c22c457376") or SHA1 in~ ("9f1f11a708d393e0a4109ae189bc64f1f3e312653dcf317a2bd406f18ffcc507", "96fa6a7714670823c83099ea01d24d6d3ae8fef027f01a4ddac14f123b1c9974", "90b1456cdbe6bc2779ea0b4736ed9a998a71ae37390331b6ba87e389a49d3d59", "e60ab99da105ee27ee09ea64ed8eb46d8edc92ee37f039dbc3e2bb9f587a33ba", "a31f222fc283227f5e7988d1ad9c0aecd66d58bb7b4d8518ae23e110308dbf91", "2915b3f8b703eb744fc54c81f4a9c67f", "aac3165ece2959f39ff98334618d10d9", "c2efb2dcacba6d3ccc175b6ce1b7ed0a", "dbd8dbecaa80795c135137d69921fdba", "7bdbd180c081fa63ca94f9c22c457376") or MD5 in~ ("9f1f11a708d393e0a4109ae189bc64f1f3e312653dcf317a2bd406f18ffcc507", "96fa6a7714670823c83099ea01d24d6d3ae8fef027f01a4ddac14f123b1c9974", "90b1456cdbe6bc2779ea0b4736ed9a998a71ae37390331b6ba87e389a49d3d59", "e60ab99da105ee27ee09ea64ed8eb46d8edc92ee37f039dbc3e2bb9f587a33ba", "a31f222fc283227f5e7988d1ad9c0aecd66d58bb7b4d8518ae23e110308dbf91", "2915b3f8b703eb744fc54c81f4a9c67f", "aac3165ece2959f39ff98334618d10d9", "c2efb2dcacba6d3ccc175b6ce1b7ed0a", "dbd8dbecaa80795c135137d69921fdba", "7bdbd180c081fa63ca94f9c22c457376")

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["9f1f11a708d393e0a4109ae189bc64f1f3e312653dcf317a2bd406f18ffcc507", "96fa6a7714670823c83099ea01d24d6d3ae8fef027f01a4ddac14f123b1c9974", "90b1456cdbe6bc2779ea0b4736ed9a998a71ae37390331b6ba87e389a49d3d59", "e60ab99da105ee27ee09ea64ed8eb46d8edc92ee37f039dbc3e2bb9f587a33ba", "a31f222fc283227f5e7988d1ad9c0aecd66d58bb7b4d8518ae23e110308dbf91", "2915b3f8b703eb744fc54c81f4a9c67f", "aac3165ece2959f39ff98334618d10d9", "c2efb2dcacba6d3ccc175b6ce1b7ed0a", "dbd8dbecaa80795c135137d69921fdba", "7bdbd180c081fa63ca94f9c22c457376"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: File or Process Matching Article Hash IOCs

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Malformed CL-STA-1132 attacker User-Agent (Mozilla/5.5 + Safari/532.31) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Threat Brief: Exploitation of PAN-OS Captive Portal Zero-Day for Unauthenticated
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] CL-STA-1132 EarthWorm staging download from 146.70.100.69:8000/php_sess · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] PAN-OS firewall service account LDAP enumeration of DomainDnsZones · LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] CL-STA-1132 EarthWorm staging download from 146.70.100.69:8000/php_sess Command & Control High
Hunts outbound HTTP fetches to the CL-STA-1132 EarthWorm staging URL (hxxp://146.70.100.69:8000/php_sess) and to the campaign's other named C2/staging IPs (67.206.213.86, 136.0.8.48, 149.104.66.84). Fires when a compromised PAN-OS firewall — or any internal host laterally pivoted from one — calls back to the attacker's HTTP file-server to retrieve the EarthWorm tunneler. Article-specific because the IP+port+URI triple is unique to this campaign. Rationale: IP triple 146.70.100.69:8000 + URI /php_sess is named explicitly in the Unit 42 brief as the EarthWorm download endpoint — not a generic-noise infrastructure. The other three IPs are listed as direct C2/attacker IPs in the article's IOC table. GitHub Acebond/ReverseSocks5 release URL is also article-named.
ATT&CKT1105 T1090.002 T1572

   or (RemoteUrl has_any ("php_sess","Acebond/ReverseSocks5")
       and RemoteIPType == "Public")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, DeviceId,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessAccountName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          ActionType, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] Malformed CL-STA-1132 attacker User-Agent (Mozilla/5.5 + Safari/532.31) Exploitation High
Hunts the malformed User-Agent the CL-STA-1132 operator used when interacting with the PAN-OS Captive Portal. The string is a forgery — real browsers emit `Mozilla/5.0` (not 5.5) and don't lead with `Safari/532.31`. This UA appearing against any web-facing surface is high-fidelity for either a captive-portal exploit attempt or follow-on operator interaction. Rationale: The UA string `Safari/532.31 Mozilla/5.5 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 ... Chrome/138.0.0.0 Safari/537.36 Edg/138.0.0.0` is reproduced verbatim in the article's IOC table. Both `Mozilla/5.5` (no real browser ships this) and a leading `Safari/532.31` token (real Safari UAs don't lead) make the AND-pair effectively unique to this operator's tooling — a first-time-seen elsewhere is high-fidelity.
ATT&CKT1190 T1071.001
Data sourcesWeb.Web
// Defender XDR has limited HTTP User-Agent visibility outside DeviceNetworkEvents.AdditionalFields
// for HttpConnectionInspected actions; this is best-effort. Pivot to firewall logs in Sentinel for primary coverage.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType == "HttpConnectionInspected"
| extend UA = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).user_agent)
| where UA has "Mozilla/5.5" and UA has "Safari/532.31"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort, UA,
          InitiatingProcessFileName
let MalformedUA1 = "Mozilla/5.5";
let MalformedUA2 = "Safari/532.31";
(union isfuzzy=true
  ( CommonSecurityLog
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where RequestClientApplication has MalformedUA1
         and RequestClientApplication has MalformedUA2
      | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, DestinationIP, DestinationPort,
                RequestURL, RequestClientApplication, DeviceVendor,
                DeviceProduct, Activity, _Source="CommonSecurityLog"),
  ( W3CIISLog
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where csUserAgent has MalformedUA1
         and csUserAgent has MalformedUA2
      | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP=cIP, DestinationIP=Computer,
                RequestURL=csUriStem, RequestClientApplication=csUserAgent,
                scStatus, _Source="W3CIISLog"))
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] PAN-OS firewall service account LDAP enumeration of DomainDnsZones Actions on Objectives Medium
Hunts the post-exploitation AD recon stage of CL-STA-1132 — using credentials lifted from the compromised firewall to query domain root and DomainDnsZones via LDAP from the firewall's management IP. The article specifies these enumeration targets and the use of the firewall's service-account credentials as the recon vector. Detection joins LDAP/SAMR query telemetry on the service account name from a non-standard / firewall source IP. Rationale: Article explicitly states the operator used `the firewall's service account credentials to target domain root and DomainDnsZones` after compromising PAN-OS. ObjectName-based 4662 with DomainDnsZones is article-named; the SubjectUserName pattern list is the standard PAN-OS LDAP-bind account naming convention (User-ID agents). Sigma omitted because Sigma's logsource model can't cleanly express a 4662-with-ObjectName-substring rule combined with the account-name pattern AND the threshold/aggregation across DCs without backend-specific extensions.
ATT&CKT1087.002 T1018 T1069.002
Data sourcesAuthentication.Authentication Endpoint.Processes
// Adjust the FirewallSvcAccounts list to your tenant's PAN-OS User-ID / LDAP bind accounts
let FirewallSvcAccounts = dynamic(["panos-svc","panfw","pa-userid","paloalto-bind","fw-svc"]);
let FirewallMgmtIPs   = dynamic([]);  // populate with PAN-OS management IPs
IdentityQueryEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("LDAP query","LDAPQuery","SAMR query")
| where Query has_any ("DomainDnsZones","DC=DomainDnsZones","CN=MicrosoftDNS")
   or QueryTarget has "DomainDnsZones"
| extend AcctLower = tolower(AccountName)
| where AcctLower in (FirewallSvcAccounts)
   or IPAddress in (FirewallMgmtIPs)
   or AccountName has_any ("panos","panfw","paloalto","user-id")
| project Timestamp, AccountName, AccountDomain, AccountUpn,
          DeviceName, IPAddress, ActionType, Query, QueryTarget, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
// 4662 = Directory Service Access; AccessMask 0x100 = property read
let FirewallSvcAccountPatterns = dynamic(["panos","panfw","paloalto","pa-userid","user-id","fw-svc"]);
let FirewallMgmtIPs = dynamic([]); // populate with PAN-OS management/data-plane IPs
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4662
| where ObjectName has_any ("DomainDnsZones","DC=DomainDnsZones","MicrosoftDNS")
   or AccessMask has "0x100"
| extend SubjectLower = tolower(SubjectUserName)
| where SubjectLower has_any (FirewallSvcAccountPatterns)
   or IpAddress in (FirewallMgmtIPs)
| summarize ObjectsAccessed = make_set(ObjectName, 25),
            QueryCount = count(),
            FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated),
            LastSeen  = max(TimeGenerated),
            DCs = make_set(Computer)
            by SubjectUserName, SubjectDomainName, IpAddress
| where QueryCount >= 5 or array_length(ObjectsAccessed) >= 3
| order by LastSeen desc
source:windows.security @EventID:4662 AND (@ObjectName:(*DomainDnsZones* OR *MicrosoftDNS*)) AND @SubjectUserName:(*panos* OR *PANOS* OR *panfw* OR *PANFW* OR *paloalto* OR *PaloAlto* OR *user-id* OR *User-ID* OR *fw-svc* OR *FW-SVC*)
ENDPOINT
`wineventlog_security` EventCode=4662 ObjectName="*DomainDnsZones*" OR ObjectName="*CN=Configuration*" OR ObjectName="*DC=*" 
| eval is_fw_account=if(match(SubjectUserName,"(?i)^(panos|panfw|pa-svc|paloalto|fw-svc|user-id).*"),1,0)
| stats min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime count values(ObjectName) as objects values(IpAddress) as src_ip values(Computer) as dc by SubjectUserName SubjectDomainName is_fw_account
| where count > 5 OR is_fw_account=1
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-0300")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-0300")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or SHA1 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or MD5 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or SHA1 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or MD5 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,

[LLM] Copy Fail (CVE-2026-31431) PoC retrieval from copy.fail/exp Delivery High
Detects download of the public Copy Fail proof-of-concept from the copy.fail domain (the URL hard-coded in Xint.Code's published 732-byte Python PoC). Hits curl/wget/python invocations referencing copy.fail in argv. Article-specific because the domain is unique to this CVE; almost any hit is exploitation prep. Rationale: Pivots on the literal string 'copy.fail' which is unique to Xint.Code's published PoC URL referenced in Unit 42's own XQL hunt query (article body, second XQL block: `action_process_image_command_line contains "copy.fail/exp"`). Domain has no legitimate use, so any curl/wget/python referencing it is high-fidelity exploitation prep. [LLM] Copy Fail (CVE-2026-31431) - Python interpreter spawning su/sudo/passwd as non-root Exploitation Medium
Detects the documented Copy Fail exploitation pattern: a Python interpreter (the 732-byte PoC script that uses only os/socket/zlib stdlib) spawning su, sudo, or passwd from an unprivileged session. Article-specific because the public PoC is Python-only and the kernel page-cache overwrite must be followed by an immediate exec of the targeted setuid binary to gain UID 0. Complements Unit 42's own Cortex XDR rule by explicitly naming python* parents (their query excludes shells/sudo/su but does not pin python as the high-signal parent). Rationale: Combines two article-specific facts: (1) the public PoC is described as a 732-byte Python script using only stdlib, so the parent of the privilege-escalating su/sudo/passwd call will be a python* binary on virtually all opportunistic exploitation; (2) the kernel-side page-cache modification only matters if the corrupted setuid binary is then exec'd. Unit 42's own published XQL excludes shells/sudo/su as parents but does not pin python — adding the python parent constraint sharpens precision considerably while still being executable on Linux endpoints with EDR, Sysmon-for-Linux, or auditd. Datadog query is parent-less because Datadog's auditd standard schema doesn't ship parent process — it captures the non-root su exec component only and should be paired with an aggregation rule that joins to a parent EXECVE within seconds, or upgraded if the tenant ships Cloud Workload Security events. Cross-checked against: • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/cve-2026-31431-copy-fail/
ATT&CKT1068 T1611 T1548.003

| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc

         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Article-specific behavioural hunt — UAT-8302 and its box full of malware
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] UAT-8302 NetDraft persistence scheduled task (Appunion.exe under \ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\) · [LLM] CloudSorcerer v3 mspdb60.dll side-load via Yandex.exe / VMtools.exe with -r -p:*.ini -s: command line · Scheduled task created with suspicious image / encoded args
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] UAT-8302 NetDraft persistence scheduled task (Appunion.exe under \ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\) Installation High
Hunts for the schtasks.exe invocation NetDraft uses to gain reboot persistence after initial implantation. The task name impersonates Microsoft\Windows\Maps with a hard-coded UUID-like string and points at C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe — both signatures are specific to this UAT-8302 / LongNosedGoblin tradecraft and not produced by any legitimate Windows component. Rationale: The persistence command is quoted verbatim in the Talos report (`schtasks /create /ru system /tn Microsoft\Windows\Maps\{a086ff1e-d6dc-45f7-b3e4-6udknw82sa} /tr 'C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe' /F`). The double `Microsoft\Microsoft\` directory and the malformed-GUID task name are unique fingerprints — no legitimate Windows installer creates that path. ESET's LongNosedGoblin write-up confirms NosyDoor uses scheduled-task persistence in the same cluster. Cross-checked against: • https://blog.talosintelligence.com/uat-8302/ • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/longnosedgoblin-tries-sniff-out-governmental-affairs-southeast-asia-japan/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/05/china-linked-uat-8302-targets.html
ATT&CKT1053.005 T1036.005 T1574.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName =~ "schtasks.exe"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "/create"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any (
    "Appunion.exe",
    @"\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\",
    "a086ff1e-d6dc-45f7-b3e4-6udknw82sa")
   or (ProcessCommandLine has @"Microsoft\Windows\Maps\" and ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\{[a-f0-9\-]{30,}\}")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentProc = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          IsRemoteSession = InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\schtasks.exe"
| where CommandLine has "/create"
| where CommandLine has_any (
    "Appunion.exe",
    @"\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\",
    "a086ff1e-d6dc-45f7-b3e4-6udknw82sa")
   or (CommandLine has @"Microsoft\Windows\Maps\" and CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\{[a-f0-9\-]{30,}\}")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, TokenElevationType

[LLM] UAT-8302 whatpc.ps1 host-recon script and ReconLiteDebug/RunWhatPC scheduled tasks Reconnaissance High
Hunts for the actor's bespoke recon harness: a PowerShell file `whatpc.ps1` dropped to C:\Windows\Temp and persisted via scheduled tasks named ReconLiteDebug or RunWhatPC. The script and task names are unique to UAT-8302 operations and surface in process telemetry whether the binary runs interactively or via schtasks. Rationale: Talos quotes the exact invocations: `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -WindowStyle Hidden -File C:\Windows\Temp\whatpc.ps1` and `schtasks /create /tn 'ReconLiteDebug' ...` / `schtasks /create /tn 'RunWhatPC' ...`. These three strings are unique to UAT-8302's recon harness — `whatpc.ps1` does not match any common red-team or sysadmin tooling, and the task names are observed across multiple intrusions in the same cluster. Cross-checked against: • https://blog.talosintelligence.com/uat-8302/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/05/china-linked-uat-8302-targets.html
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1053.005 T1082 T1087.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let recon_strings = dynamic(["whatpc.ps1", "ReconLiteDebug", "RunWhatPC"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any (recon_strings)
   or (FileName =~ "schtasks.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has @"c:\windows\temp\run.bat" and ProcessCommandLine has "/ru SYSTEM")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          IsRemote = AdditionalFields
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] CloudSorcerer v3 mspdb60.dll side-load via Yandex.exe / VMtools.exe with -r -p:*.ini -s: command line Installation High
Hunts the UAT-8302 CloudSorcerer v3 sideload triad: a benign-named launcher (Yandex.exe or VMtools.exe) executed with the unique flag pattern `-r -p:<file>.ini -s:<n>` that side-loads a hijacked mspdb60.dll which decrypts the .ini-encoded shellcode. The combination of binary name, distinctive cmdline, and mspdb60.dll image-load is not produced by any legitimate Yandex or VMware product. Rationale: Talos directly quotes the loader invocations `Yandex.exe -r -p:test.ini -s:12` and `VMtools.exe -r -p:VM.ini -s:12` and names mspdb60.dll as the malicious side-loaded DLL that decrypts the .ini-encoded CloudSorcerer v3 shellcode. The flag pattern `-r -p:*.ini -s:<digit>` is bespoke to this loader (legitimate Yandex Browser / VMware Tools binaries do not accept these arguments) and pairing it with mspdb60.dll loaded from a non-system path makes false positives extremely rare. Cross-checked against Kaspersky's original CloudSorcerer disclosure and the EastWind campaign analysis, which document the same sideload-triad TTP with renamed signed binaries. Cross-checked against: • https://blog.talosintelligence.com/uat-8302/ • https://securelist.com/cloudsorcerer-new-apt-cloud-actor/113056/ • https://securelist.com/eastwind-apt-campaign/113345/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/cloudsorcerer-hackers-abuse-cloud-services-to-steal-russian-govt-data/
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1027.013 T1055
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
// Stage 1 — distinctive launcher cmdline pattern
let sideload_procs = DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName in~ ("Yandex.exe", "VMtools.exe")
    | where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-r\s+-p:\S+\.ini\s+-s:\d+"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, AccountName,
              FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
              ProcessCommandLine, ProcessId;
// Stage 2 — mspdb60.dll image-loaded by the same process (DLL hijack confirmation)
let hijack_loads = DeviceImageLoadEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName =~ "mspdb60.dll"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("Yandex.exe","VMtools.exe")
    | project LoadTime = Timestamp, DeviceId, ProcessId = InitiatingProcessId,
              LoadedDll = FolderPath, DllSHA256 = SHA256;
sideload_procs
| join kind=inner hijack_loads on DeviceId, ProcessId
| where LoadTime between (Timestamp .. Timestamp + 5m)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
          ProcessCommandLine, LoadedDll, DllSHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
// Sentinel cannot observe image-loads without Sysmon, so we anchor on the
// distinctive launcher cmdline pattern alone (rare enough to alert on).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\Yandex.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\VMtools.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-r\s+-p:\S+\.ini\s+-s:\d+"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, TokenElevationType
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 4f1d8a35-ecb2-4791-9e10-a64f9d5a7ce1
description: Detects the CloudSorcerer v3 DLL side-loading triad used by UAT-8302 — a benign-named launcher (Yandex.exe or VMtools.exe) loading mspdb60.dll from a non-system path.
references:

logsource:
  category: image_load
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\Yandex.exe'
      - '\VMtools.exe'
    ImageLoaded|endswith: '\mspdb60.dll'

      - 'C:\Program Files\'
      - 'C:\Program Files (x86)\'
      - 'C:\Windows\System32\'
      - 'C:\Windows\SysWOW64\'
  condition: selection and not filter_legit
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:7 @ImageLoaded:*\\mspdb60.dll @Image:(*\\Yandex.exe OR *\\VMtools.exe) -@ImageLoaded:(C\:\\Program\ Files\\* OR C\:\\Windows\\System32\\* OR C\:\\Windows\\SysWOW64\\*)

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

Article-specific behavioural hunt — UAT-8302 and its box full of malware Exploitation High
Auto-generated hunt targeting the specific binaries, paths, registry locations and command-line fragments named in this article. Hunting tier — needs analyst review and environment tuning before promotion to alerting.

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Registry
// Article-specific bespoke detection — UAT-8302 and its box full of malware
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("whatpc.ps1", "run.bat", "arp.exe", "route.exe", "netstat.exe", "nltest.exe", "httpx.exe", "adconnectdump.py", "ae.exe", "7zr.exe", "e1.bat", "appunion.exe", "yandex.exe", "vmtools.exe", "dpapimig.exe") or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("-WindowStyle Hidden", "certutil -urlcache") or FolderPath has_any ("C:\Windows\Temp\whatpc.ps1", "C:\Windows\Temp\alive_hosts.txt", "C:\Windows\Temp\portscan.txt", "C:\ProgramData\S.exe", "C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FolderPath has_any ("C:\Windows\Temp\whatpc.ps1", "C:\Windows\Temp\alive_hosts.txt", "C:\Windows\Temp\portscan.txt", "C:\ProgramData\S.exe", "C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe") or FileName in~ ("whatpc.ps1", "run.bat", "arp.exe", "route.exe", "netstat.exe", "nltest.exe", "httpx.exe", "adconnectdump.py", "ae.exe", "7zr.exe", "e1.bat", "appunion.exe", "yandex.exe", "vmtools.exe", "dpapimig.exe"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

    where (Processes.process_name IN ("whatpc.ps1","run.bat","arp.exe","route.exe","netstat.exe","nltest.exe","httpx.exe","adconnectdump.py","ae.exe","7zr.exe","e1.bat","appunion.exe","yandex.exe","vmtools.exe","dpapimig.exe") OR Processes.process="*-WindowStyle Hidden*" OR Processes.process="*certutil -urlcache*" OR Processes.process_path="*C:\Windows\Temp\whatpc.ps1*" OR Processes.process_path="*C:\Windows\Temp\alive_hosts.txt*" OR Processes.process_path="*C:\Windows\Temp\portscan.txt*" OR Processes.process_path="*C:\ProgramData\S.exe*" OR Processes.process_path="*C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name,
       Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_path
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)`
| append [
| tstats summariesonly=false count
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("created","modified")
      AND (Filesystem.file_path="*C:\Windows\Temp\whatpc.ps1*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*C:\Windows\Temp\alive_hosts.txt*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*C:\Windows\Temp\portscan.txt*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*C:\ProgramData\S.exe*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe*" OR Filesystem.file_name IN ("whatpc.ps1","run.bat","arp.exe","route.exe","netstat.exe","nltest.exe","httpx.exe","adconnectdump.py","ae.exe","7zr.exe","e1.bat","appunion.exe","yandex.exe","vmtools.exe","dpapimig.exe"))
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.process_name,
       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
]

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Shai-Hulud Worm Pivots to Multi-Cloud: intercom-client@7.0.4 Hijacked — 361,000
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Shai-Hulud npm preinstall: node spawns Bun runtime from bun-dl-* tmpdir · [LLM] Shai-Hulud AI coding-agent persistence: .claude/settings.json + .vscode/tasks.json drops · [LLM] Shai-Hulud known-bad setup.mjs / execution.js SHA256 hash match · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) · OAuth consent / suspicious app grant
[LLM] Shai-Hulud npm preinstall: node spawns Bun runtime from bun-dl-* tmpdir Installation High
Detects the Shai-Hulud worm's signature execution chain where the malicious npm preinstall hook (node setup.mjs) downloads the Bun JavaScript runtime to a mkdtempSync-created directory prefixed bun-dl- and then executes execution.js under Bun to evade EDRs that only watch for node children of npm install. Targets the exact loader behaviour in setup.mjs (SHA256 4066781f...) shared across mbt@1.2.48 and the three compromised @cap-js packages. Rationale: Pivots on the exact mkdtempSync prefix 'bun-dl-' and the platform-specific Bun release-asset names (bun-windows-x64-baseline, bun-linux-x64-baseline, bun-linux-x64-musl-baseline, bun-linux-aarch64, bun-darwin) hard-coded in setup.mjs (SHA256 4066781fa830224c8bbcc3aa005a396657f9c8f9016f9a64ad44a9d7f5f45e34), plus the literal 'execution.js' filename — the article calls out 'a different binary name that bypasses' EDR monitors of node children, which is the exact behaviour this UC flips back into a detection. IOCs taken directly from StepSecurity's runtime trace.
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007 T1027.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
| where (FileName in~ ("bun","bun.exe")
      or FolderPath has "bun-dl-"
      or FolderPath has "bun-windows-x64-baseline"
      or FolderPath has "bun-linux-x64-baseline"
      or FolderPath has "bun-linux-x64-musl-baseline"
      or FolderPath has "bun-linux-aarch64"
      or FolderPath has "bun-darwin"
      or ProcessCommandLine has "execution.js")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("setup.mjs","preinstall","npm install","yarn install","pnpm install")
   or FolderPath has "bun-dl-"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256, InitiatingProcessSHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ParentProcessName in~ ("node.exe","node")
| where TargetProcessName in~ ("bun","bun.exe")
   or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("bun-dl-","bun-windows-x64-baseline","bun-linux-x64-baseline","bun-linux-x64-musl-baseline","bun-linux-aarch64","bun-darwin","execution.js")
| where ParentProcessCommandLine has_any ("setup.mjs","preinstall","npm install","yarn install","pnpm install")
   or TargetProcessCommandLine has "bun-dl-"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine,
          TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Shai-Hulud npm Preinstall Drops and Executes Bun Runtime from Temp Directory
id: 9b4e7c12-2f1a-4d3b-9a6c-7c8e1b54a013
description: Detects the Shai-Hulud supply-chain worm's loader (setup.mjs) downloading the Bun JavaScript runtime to a mkdtempSync-created directory prefixed 'bun-dl-' under the system temp path and executing the obfuscated execution.js credential stealer under Bun to evade node-focused EDR monitoring.
references:
    - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/shai-hulud-worm-pivots-to-multi-cloud-intercom-client-hijacked
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/04/30
tags:
    - attack.t1195.002
    - attack.t1059.007

    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\node.exe'
            - '/node'

        - Image|contains: 'bun-dl-'

            - '\bun.exe'

level: high

[LLM] Shai-Hulud AI coding-agent persistence: .claude/settings.json + .vscode/tasks.json drops Installation High
Detects the Shai-Hulud worm's novel persistence mechanism where the payload commits or writes .claude/settings.json (abusing Claude Code's SessionStart hook) and .vscode/tasks.json (with runOn:folderOpen) into every accessible repository. Any developer re-opening the repo in VSCode or Claude Code silently re-executes the malware — this is one of the first supply-chain campaigns weaponising AI-agent config files. Rationale: The article describes 'one of the first supply chain attacks to target AI coding agent configurations as a persistence and propagation vector', specifically dropping .claude/settings.json (SessionStart hook abuse) and .vscode/tasks.json with runOn:folderOpen. Detection bucket-aggregates both filenames in a 1h window because the loader writes them as a pair, and falls back to the suspect-parent process list (node/bun/git) when only one of the two files is observed — that combination is essentially nil in legitimate developer activity. Sigma omitted because the detection is a multi-file correlation, not a single event.
ATT&CKT1546 T1195.002 T1554
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
let _window = 1h;
let _suspect_files = DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
    | where (FolderPath endswith @"\.claude\settings.json"
          or FolderPath endswith "/.claude/settings.json"
          or FolderPath endswith @"\.vscode\tasks.json"
          or FolderPath endswith "/.vscode/tasks.json")
    | extend FileKind = case(
        FolderPath has ".claude", "claude_settings",
        FolderPath has ".vscode", "vscode_tasks",
        "other")
    | extend Bucket = bin(Timestamp, _window);
_suspect_files
| summarize FilesDropped = make_set(FileKind),
            FirstSeen = min(Timestamp),
            LastSeen = max(Timestamp),
            Paths = make_set(FolderPath, 50),
            Initiators = make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName, 20),
            InitiatingCmds = make_set(InitiatingProcessCommandLine, 20)
            by DeviceId, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, Bucket
| where array_length(FilesDropped) >= 2
   or Initiators has_any ("node.exe","node","bun.exe","bun","git.exe","git")
| order by FirstSeen desc
let _window = 1h;
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (TargetFilePath endswith @"\.claude\settings.json"
      or TargetFilePath endswith "/.claude/settings.json"
      or TargetFilePath endswith @"\.vscode\tasks.json"
      or TargetFilePath endswith "/.vscode/tasks.json")
| extend FileKind = case(
    TargetFilePath has ".claude", "claude_settings",
    TargetFilePath has ".vscode", "vscode_tasks",
    "other")
| extend Bucket = bin(TimeGenerated, _window)
| summarize FilesDropped = make_set(FileKind),
            FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated),
            Paths = make_set(TargetFilePath, 50),
            Actors = make_set(ActorUsername, 20),
            ActingProcs = make_set(ActingProcessName, 20),
            ActingCmds = make_set(ActingProcessCommandLine, 20)
            by DvcHostname, Bucket
| where array_length(FilesDropped) >= 2
   or ActingProcs has_any ("node.exe","node","bun.exe","bun","git.exe","git")
| order by FirstSeen desc

ENDPOINT

[LLM] Shai-Hulud known-bad setup.mjs / execution.js SHA256 hash match Installation High
Detects on-disk or in-execution presence of the two Shai-Hulud loader/payload files by their byte-identical SHA256 hashes — the setup.mjs loader (4066781f...) is identical across all four confirmed compromised packages (mbt@1.2.48, @cap-js/sqlite@2.2.2, @cap-js/postgres@v2.2.2, @cap-js/db-service@v2.10.1), and execution.js (80a3d287...) is the 11.6 MB obfuscated credential stealer. This is the highest-fidelity retrospective hunt across npm-using hosts and CI runners. Rationale: The article explicitly states setup.mjs is 'SHA-256 (identical across all compromised packages) 4066781fa830224c8bbcc3aa005a396657f9c8f9016f9a64ad44a9d7f5f45e34' and execution.js for mbt@1.2.48 is 80a3d2877813968ef847ae73b5eeeb70b9435254e74d7f07d8cf4057f0a710ac. These are the highest-fidelity IOCs in the campaign — byte-identical across four packages confirms the same operator/toolchain. Union across file, process and image-load tables ensures retrospective coverage whether the artifact landed on disk only, was executed, or was loaded.
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes
let _ShaiHulud_Hashes = dynamic([
    "4066781fa830224c8bbcc3aa005a396657f9c8f9016f9a64ad44a9d7f5f45e34",  // setup.mjs (loader, identical across all 4 compromised packages)
    "80a3d2877813968ef847ae73b5eeeb70b9435254e74d7f07d8cf4057f0a710ac"   // execution.js (11.6MB obfuscated payload from mbt@1.2.48)
]);
union isfuzzy=true
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
        | where SHA256 in (_ShaiHulud_Hashes)
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
                  InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
        | where SHA256 in (_ShaiHulud_Hashes) or InitiatingProcessSHA256 in (_ShaiHulud_Hashes)
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType="ProcessCreated", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
                  InitiatingProcessAccountName=AccountName, Source="DeviceProcessEvents" ),
    ( DeviceImageLoadEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
        | where SHA256 in (_ShaiHulud_Hashes)
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType="ImageLoaded", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine="",
                  InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="DeviceImageLoadEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc
let _ShaiHulud_Hashes = dynamic([
    "4066781fa830224c8bbcc3aa005a396657f9c8f9016f9a64ad44a9d7f5f45e34",
    "80a3d2877813968ef847ae73b5eeeb70b9435254e74d7f07d8cf4057f0a710ac"
]);
union isfuzzy=true
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in (_ShaiHulud_Hashes) or Hash in (_ShaiHulud_Hashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType, TargetFileName, TargetFilePath,
                  Hash = coalesce(TargetFileSHA256, Hash), ActingProcessName, ActorUsername,
                  Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
        | where SHA256 in (_ShaiHulud_Hashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType="ProcessCreated",
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  Hash = SHA256, ActingProcessName = ParentProcessName,
                  ActorUsername, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
